### 2. Date and numeral disagreements with the archive

**Twelve recorded. In every one the book's reading stands in the notes and the archive's is named
only for comparison; nothing has been reconciled, and no third value appears anywhere below.**

**Carry this warning with the whole section.** Ten of the twelve are printed in, or corroborated by,
the two index chunks — chunk 027, which its own worker calls **"the worst-damaged chunk of the
seventeen"**, and chunk 028, measured as **"the second worst"**, where the damage *"lands on exactly
the part of the text that matters least safely: the locators."* Chunk 028's table gives no
per-entry page reference; its entries are printed across the `INDEX OF NAMES AND PLACES`,
pp. 518–527.

**And carry this second warning, which is the one most likely to be forgotten.** Where a death year
appears both in the book's running text and in the book's own index, **that is the same book twice,
not two books.** It is not corroboration. This affects Sachal Sarmast, Jalāluddīn Surkhpūsh and
Makhdūm-i Jahāniyān above all, and also Farīduddīn Ganj-i Shakar, Raḥmān Bābā and Makhdūm Nūḥ.

| # | figure / archive id | the book prints | where | the archive |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **shrine-of-makhdoom-jahaniyan-jahangasht** | `Jalaluddln Makhdum-i Jahaniyan (d. 1382-83)` | index, chunk 028 (pp. 518–527) — and `d. 1383` in the running text at p. 385, folio 354 | 1384 |
| 2 | **shrine-of-jalaluddin-surkh-posh-bukhari-jalaluddin-bukhari** | `Jalaluddln Surkhpush (d. 1292)` | index, chunk 028 — and `d. 1292` in the running text at p. 385, folio 354 | 1291 |
| 3 | **lal-shahbaz-qalandar** | `Lai Shahbaz Qalandar (d. 1262)` | index, chunk 028 | 1274 |
| 4 | **madho-lal-hussain** | `Madho Lai Husayn (d. 1593)` | index, chunk 028 | 1599 |
| 5 | **tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak** | `Qutbuddln Aybek (d. 1206)` | index, chunk 028 | 1210 |
| 6 | **shrine-of-sachal-sarmast** | `Sachal Sarmast (d. 1826)` | index, chunk 028 — and `Sachal Sarmast (i 739-1826)` in the running text at p. 424 | 1827 |
| 7 | **shrine-of-makhdoom-nooh-hala** | `Makhdum Nuh (i7th century)` [text-layer?: 17th century] | p. 520, folio 489 — and `in the seventeenth century` in the running text at p. 421 | 1590 |
| 8 | **rahman-baba-mausoleum-rehman-baba-shrine** | `^bdu^r-Rahman (Rahman Baba)` `(d. 1709)` | p. 510, folio 479 — and `(d. 1709)` in the running text at p. 430 | 1711 |
| 9 | **mazar-of-bulleh-shah** | `Bullhe Shah` `(d. 1752)` | p. 513, folio 482 | 1757 |
| 10 | **shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar** | `Fariduddin Ganj-i Shakar` `(d. 1265)` | p. 514, folio 483 — and `d. 1265` in the running text at p. 377, folio 346 | 1266 |
| 11 | **data-darbar** | `Hujwiri (d. circa 1071)`; clean `1071`; index `(d. ca. 1071)` | p. 37, folio 6 (chunk 001); p. 119, folio 88 (chunk 006); chunk 027 | 1072 |
| 12 | **shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya** | `d. ca. 1262` in the running text; `Baha^uddln Zakariya Multani (d. ca.` / `1267)` in the index | p. 383, folio 352 (chunk 020); chunk 027 | 1267 |

**Three of these need saying out loud.**

- **Lāl Shahbāz Qalandar, 1262 against 1274, is the widest gap in the table** — twelve years, on the
  principal figure of the book's single richest shrine passage (the Sehwān fair, the lingam, the
  malang, pp. 386, folio 355). It is printed only in the index, in the second-worst-damaged chunk,
  and nowhere in the running text, where chunk 020's last sentence breaks off mid-clause at `its
  representative is Lai Shahbaz,` (p. 385, folio 354) with no date at all.
- **Bullhe Shah's `1752` is suspect on its face.** Two pages away in the same index,
  `Abdu3l-Lat!f Bhita3!, Shah` is printed `(d. 1752)` — **the identical year** — in a chunk where
  chunk 027 measures **`!` standing for `1` ten times**. The archive's Shāh ʿAbduʾl-Laṭīf row
  (**bhit-bhit-shah**) does carry 1752, so one of the two readings is right and the other may be a
  digit that migrated. Do not let `1752` into a Bulleh Shah entry without the page image.
- **Item 12 is an internal contradiction, not a book-against-archive one.** The book's running text
  gives Bahāʾuddīn Zakariyā `d. ca. 1262` and the book's own index gives `d. ca. 1267`. The index
  agrees with the archive; the running text does not. Both are hedged by the book with `ca.` Chunk
  020 records the four printed years in its range — `1265`, `ca. 1262`, `1292`, `1383` — as **clean
  in the text layer**, i.e. genuine source readings and not damage.

**Three further numeral points that are not disagreements and must not become them.**

- **darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz** — no date in this book at all. The archive's `8 August 1041, as given in
  the account; calendar era unstated` appears nowhere in it. Chunk 017 warns explicitly that the
  `(d. 1030)` on p. 323, folio 292 is **Maḥmūd of Ghazna's, not Ayāz's**, and that neither date has
  been carried onto the other.
- **shrine-of-makhdoom-abdul-rahim-girhori** — no death year from this book either. Chunk 022:
  *"Schimmel also gives no death year for him, so the row's year is neither confirmed nor
  contradicted."* What the book does give is a **silsila conflict**: she calls him `the
  eighteenth-century Suhrawardi mystic` (p. 418, folio 387) where the archive row records a
  different order.
- **A near-identity refused on the archive's own data.** `Miran Husain Shah (d. 1669), 296` (p. 521,
  folio `49°` [text-layer?: 490]) was checked against **shrine-of-miran-hussain-zanjani-zanjani-sahib**
  (`figure_died` 1042) and refused by chunk 028: *"the death years are six centuries apart and the
  book gives `Shah`, not `Zanjani`."*

**Two agreements, recorded for contrast so a reviewer knows the book is not simply wrong.**
`lnayat Shahid, Shah (d. 1718)` (p. 517, folio 486) matches **dargah-roza-sufi-shah-inayat-shaheed**'s
1718; `Shah cAbduDl-Latif in Sind (d. 1752)` (p. 200, folio 169) matches **bhit-bhit-shah**'s 1752 —
with the Bullhe Shah caveat above attached.

---

### 3. The damage classes, inventoried once each

Route is `text_layer`; every flag is `[text-layer?]`; there is **not one typographic ligature
character (`ﬁ`, `ﬂ`, `ﬀ`) anywhere in this book**, so the total-and-mechanical exemption never
applied and every damaged token carries its own inline flag. **No original Arabic, Persian, Urdu,
Sindhi or Panjabi script is printed anywhere in the book** — every oriental term appears only in the
book's own damaged romanisation, which is an explicit negative repeated in chunk after chunk and
means no date or name can be checked against an original script. The book's own transcription table
(pp. 28–29, folios XXV–XXVI) has an empty `Letter in basic form` column on all thirty-two rows.

#### 3.1 The `fi`/`fl` ligature loss that produces REAL ENGLISH WORDS — the most dangerous class

Nothing looks wrong on the page. Do not "correct" one silently, and never let one become a place.

- **`Sanaa's` = Sanāʾī's, eleven times in chunk 018** (pp. 332–334, 342, 346) — **it reads as the
  name of a city.** This is the worst instance in the book.
- `denned` = defined (p. 35; p. 174; p. 213, folio 182; p. 235, folio 204). `Suns` = Sufis (p. 169).
  `Sunsm` = Sufism (p. 54, folio 23; p. 434). `to fmd` = to find (p. 210, folio 179).
  `shark` = sharḥ (p. 208, folio 177, footnote 80; p. 311, footnote 44; p. 328).
  `Ibn Sma` = Ibn Sīnā (p. 211, folio 180; p. 481). `Carnal` = ʿamal (p. 305).
  `batnroom` = bathroom (p. 203, folio 172). `rilled` = filled, inside a quoted verse (p. 109,
  folio 78). `Underbill` = Underhill (pp. 20, 35; p. 67, folio 36; p. 496).
  `marts` = morts (p. 307, footnote 40). `Tame` = l'âme (p. 328). `Hose-Garden` = Rose-Garden
  (p. 312, footnote 47). `Flurufi` = Hurufi (p. 370, folio 339). `Fusils al-hikam` = Fuṣūṣ (p. 484).
  `Sunk` = Sufik (p. 486). `Paris, Nabih A.` = Faris (p. 476). `Fine Lebensbeschreibung` = Eine
  (p. 496). `Buck der Siegelringsteine` = Buch (p. 484). `Mattiawl` / `Mathnazui` = Mathnawī
  (p. 348, folio 317). `moderns` = moderno (p. 347).
- **The reverse hazard, also real**: `nnd`-class damage that destroys a word's sense outright —
  `I left myself, and lo, no fiend was left for me` where the preceding line reads `friend`
  (p. 373, folio 342). Chunk 020: *"the couplet's point may turn on that word and it cannot be
  settled from the text layer."*

#### 3.2 `°` — a THREE-WAY (in practice four-value) ambiguity, never resolved anywhere

Both digit values are **proved inside this book's own folios**: the header `44°` on p. 471 must be
folio 440 and the header `45°` on p. 489 must be folio 458 (chunk 027). It also renders the **ʿayn**
— `°Ad, 198` [text-layer?: ʿĀd] (p. 510, folio 479), `Schah °Abdul Latif's` (p. 226, folio 195),
`cAynu°l-Qudat's` (p. 327, folio 296), `Ndla-yi °Andallb` (p. 466), `A°zam Tattawl` (p. 204,
footnote 72) — and the **letter `o`**: `l^e eYe °f certainty, 142` [text-layer?: the eye of
certainty] (p. 507, folio 476). Instances carried unresolved include `2°8` (p. 506, folio 475),
`25°`, `4°7`, `4°3` (p. 511, folio 480), `4°i`, `37°`, `39°`, `29°` (pp. 513, 517), `43°` (p. 514),
`34°` (pp. 516, 526, 530, 537), `49°` (p. 521), `3°9` (pp. 525, 531), `36°` (p. 535), `4°5`
(p. 536), `8°` (pp. 521, 538), `4°6` (p. 538), `3°6` (p. 539), `3°°` — two in one token — (p. 542),
`(L5°)` = `L 50` (p. 455, folio 424), `3°9` (p. 358, folio 327), `44°` (p. 471), `45°` (p. 489).
**Where a `°` sits in a folio read from a running header, the parity rule plus `PDF − 31` resolves
it as a cross-check. Where a `°` sits inside a LOCATOR there is no such check and none was
supplied.**

#### 3.3 `g` is 9 AND 3 — proved twice inside single chunks

- **The same printed token is two different numbers.** `lig` must be **113** on p. 144 (`g` = 3) and
  **119** on p. 150 (`g` = 9), both under `folio = PDF − 31`, and **both satisfy the odd-trailing
  parity check, so parity cannot separate them** (chunk 008). A human should confirm p. 144's folio
  against the page image.
- **Both values measured inside chunk 017.** The footnote printed `g.` at the foot of p. 321 sits
  between footnotes `2` and `4`, so there `g` = **3**; the running header of p. 329 prints `2g8`,
  where the arithmetic and the leading-even parity both make `g` = **9**.
- **`Ibn Taymiy-` / `ya (d. igaS)`** (p. 304, folio 273) is therefore **1928 or 1328** and was
  correctly left unresolved — chunk 016 calls it *"the single most consequential unresolved numeral
  in the chunk."* The same `g` ambiguity leaves `(Tehran, 1337 sh./igsS)` (p. 311, footnote 44) and
  `1342 sh./igSg` (p. 314, folio 283) unresolved.
- **Adjacent and equally dangerous: `2` prints as `a`, `s` OR `g`.** Chunk 001 states the
  consequence: *"in this book a `2` can print as `a`, `s` or `g`, so `goio` is 2010 and not 9010"*
  (Ernst's footnotes, pp. 17–20). Hence `ad ed.` / `sd ed.` / `gd ed.` are all `2d ed.`, and
  `(Hag)` (p. 154) is consistent with `H 22`, `H 29`, `H 92` **and** `H 99` — left as printed.

#### 3.4 Damaged numbers inside index locators — `no`, `in`, `n` read as English words

Chunk 029: *"Nothing looks wrong on the page, and none of these has been resolved."*

- `no` stands for a number in `Judgment, Last, … 95, no, 137` (p. 533), `Love, … 100, no, 123`
  (p. 535), `Repentance … of, 56, no` (p. 540), `Wine, no, 137` (p. 543), `Zuhd ("renunciation"),
  37, no.` (p. 543), `Persian poetry … no, in, 112` (p. 538), and in footnotes `Ibid., p. no.`
  (p. 70, folio 39; p. 425, folio 394; p. 295; p. 323).
- `in` stands for a number in `Poverty, … 100, in, 120-24` (p. 538), `Renunciation, … 95, in, 112`
  (p. 538), `Zdhid-i zahirparast … , in.` (p. 543).
- `n` stands for a number at the head of `Saints, n, 21, 72` (p. 540), in `Chinese, n, 67, 27`
  (p. 513, folio 482), `system of, n, 191` (p. 517, folio 486), `Bibi Fatima (nth century)`
  (p. 513, folio 482) and in the siglum `(H n)` = `H 11` (p. 48, folio 17).
- **`n` is ambiguous within a single line.** It is a genuine "note" marker in `9 n` (p. 533),
  `355 n` (p. 540), `127 n` and `265 n` and `39° n` (pp. 542, 543) — and a damaged digit in
  `Saints, n, 21` (p. 540). Both are left as printed.
- **Locator strings are also mis-segmented, not only mis-read.** Stops print for commas throughout:
  `113. l69- 195. 217, 234- 300` is one entry's locator with three full stops and a hyphen where
  commas belong (p. 510, folio 479). Numbers split by a space: `1 16` (p. 511), `1 1 2` and `1 10`
  (pp. 507–508), `35 1` (p. 511), `IS 8` (p. 516), `3 8l` (p. 517), `i 15` (p. 508).
- **Locator sequences printed out of ascending order**, a symptom of a misread digit, flagged and
  not corrected: `Qutb … 57, 200, 302, 203, 369` (p. 539) and `Monism, 5, 47, 64, 267, 263, 273`
  (p. 536).
- **Century forms are systematically broken** — `i7th`, `i8th`, `isth`, `i3th`, `i5th`, `<7th`,
  `gth`, `loth`, `i2th`, `i4th`: the leading `1` is lost to `i`, `<` or `l` in every one. **This
  damages `Makhdum Nuh (i7th century)`, `Lai Udero (i7th century)` and `Warith Shah (late i8th
  century)` — all three of which are dating evidence** (see §2, item 7).

#### 3.5 Right-margin truncation in the chapter text — a word cut short is not a variant spelling

Present throughout the running text, not only in the bibliography. Chunk 014 (seven): `su-`/`sidized`
= subsidized (p. 263), `r-`/`placed` (p. 262), `inherite` (p. 269), `peasan` (p. 273),
`re`/`sponsible` (p. 271), `Cam`/`bridge` (p. 272), `mu-`/`sulmani` (p. 265). Chunk 017 (five):
`think of th` (p. 313), `with Moses and wit.` (p. 313), `because "ther` (p. 314), `wisely told hi`
(p. 314), `as we. as His beauty` (p. 315). Chunk 018 (six): `as his surnam.` (p. 334, folio 303),
`More Tales from the Mathnawi (Londo.` (p. 341, folio 310), `in the norther.` (p. 343, folio 312),
`In Damascus, he may hav.` (p. 343, folio 312), `Manaqib a` (p. 346, folio 315), `"Kin Midas`
(p. 348, folio 317). Chunk 016: `haqlqa muhammadiyy` (p. 303), `good i` (p. 309), `for an
uninitiate. person` (p. 303), `Thefifteenth-centuryNaqshband` (p. 296). Chunk 015 (four): `in hono`
(p. 279, folio 248), `an` for `and` (p. 283, folio 252), `According to th` (p. 283, folio 252),
`there is no v` — probably `veil`, **not supplied** (p. 284, folio 253). Chunk 007: `whic` (p. 126),
`endow` (p. 135), `hav` (p. 137), `ana` (p. 142), plus a **left**-margin truncation in a running
header, `104   /   E PATH` (p. 135). Chunk 008: `reportedl` (p. 145, folio 114), `th bodies of the
upright` (p. 146, folio 115). Chunk 006: `th only object of love` (p. 109, folio 78), `th vulgar`
(p. 118, folio 87). Chunk 025: `It contain` (p. 469), `Lahor.` (p. 470), `In in Arabic` (p. 471).
Chunk 026, where it is worst: `Grundriss der Iranischen Philo` / `gie` (p. 476),
`Romantisch-mystisches Epos vom Sche` / `Hilali` (p. 476), `Bombay, 195` with the year cut (p. 494),
`Islamisch-arabische H e Hi genii eder` (p. 485).
**In the two index chunks the cut falls inside a LOCATOR, not a word**: `Sura 105, allusion to
Abraha, the lord o` (p. 507), `Red rose is part of God's glory, 222, 29.` and `I was a hidden
treasure, 139, 189, 26` (pp. 508–509), `Husayn ibn cAli (d. 680), 75, 340, 39` (p. 516),
`Hizb al-bahr. by Shadhili, 178, 25`, `Hurr (militant group in Sind), 396, 39`,
`Qibla (prayer direction), 195, 29`, `Muharram, 75, 340, 341 , 38`, `Hunger, … 168, 24.`,
`Proximity, … 204, 2.`, `Nafs ("lower soul"), 112-14, 116, 190, 19.` (pp. 533–543). **A cut locator
is not a short locator and none has been completed.**

#### 3.6 The colliding index columns on PDF 536 — and on PDF 519

The text layer emits each index page's two columns line by line, and where the column gap collapses
two unrelated entries run together on one physical line.

- **PDF 536** (chunk 029): `and trial, 224, 225; as letter of Mu- Mawadda ("love," "charity"), 132`
  and `Mim duasi (Bektashi prayer connected Mihrab ("prayer niche"), 293`. The worker's instruction:
  ***"a human should re-check any entry on p. 536 against the page image."*** This is the page that
  carries `Mdlang (dervish at the sanctuary in Sehwan), 355` and
  `Mela chiraghdn (festival in Lahore), 384` — two of the most quotable glosses in the book and two
  of the declines in §1.3.
- **PDF 519** (chunk 028): `Khaqani … 16,` and `Krishna, 271, 434` share line space. The worker:
  *"a human re-checking `Krishna`, `Mango Pir` and `Shiva` should confirm the column assignment,
  since all three are short entries in a crowded right column."* **Those are precisely the entries
  that carry the ten many-row attachments in §1.3.**

#### 3.7 The remaining classes, once each

- **ʿayn and hamza — the heaviest class in the book** (chunk 026 measures 272 occurrences in its
  pages alone), and **it lands on names, which is what mapping runs on**. Renderings observed across
  the book: `c`, `C`, `^`, `3`, `D`, `P`, `?`, `*`, `'`, `"`, `V`, `:`, `:)`, `>`, `\`, `•`, `f`,
  `ff`, `j`, `e`, `°`, `0`, `1`, `5`, `7`, `2`, `G`, `-'-`, `'v` and a bare hyphen. The book itself
  explains the root cause: its printed glyph for ʿayn is a superscript `c` and is sometimes
  `unnoticed` altogether (`'ayn: c, or unnoticed`, p. 29, folio XXVI). **A real `c` exists in the
  text too**, so the class is systematic but not token-reversible; no name is ever silently
  normalised.
- **A lone capital on its own line is usually a DETACHED ʿAYN, not a siglum.** Measured in chunks
  001, 003, 005, 007, 009, 010, 011, 012, 013, 014, 015, 016, 018, 021, 025, 026, 027. The clearest
  trap: a bare `G` on its own line on p. 190 (folio 159) is the ʿayn of `cAttar`, not the Ghazzālī
  siglum. In chunk 027 the detachment strips the **first character off an index headword**, so an
  entry can begin `Abdu3l-Lat!f` with its `c` on the line above. Chunk 020 records the opposite
  behaviour — the ʿayn **glues to the END of the preceding word**: `he however replied thatc this
  was prohibited`, `he went to the doorc of Iraqi's cell` (p. 384, folio 353).
- **Long vowels: `ā` → `d`, `ī` → `l` / `1` / `!` / `I` / `m` / `f`.** Pervasive, and the class that
  mangles the mapped names. Worked examples of how far it goes: `Hujwiri` prints **six** ways in two
  pages (pp. 118–120); `Muʿīnuddīn` **six** ways across five pages (pp. 376–385);
  `Baha'uddin` **six** ways across three pages (pp. 383–385), of which `BahjPuddin` (p. 385,
  folio 354) *"would not be recognisable without its context"*; `Farīduddīn` **six** ways
  (pp. 377–383); `Dhūʾn-Nūn` **six** ways in sixteen pages (chunk 004); `Ibn ʿArabī` **ten** ways in
  twenty pages (chunk 016); `Baha'uddin Naqshband` **three** ways on one page (p. 395, folio 364).
  The technical term `samāʿ` prints in **at least nine** damaged forms in chunk 011 — *"any string
  search for this word will under-count badly."*
- **`ff` for a final `āʾ`** — `Ihyff`, `auliyff`, `al-auliyff`, `mashffikh`, `Lawffih`, `Ar-rasffil`,
  `qasffid`, `thancfan`, `rajff`, `anffl-Haqq`, `illffl-^ishq`, `Nawffi`, `Tffiyya`. **`ff` is
  ambiguous**: at `pp. 7gff.` (p. 309, footnote 41) and `V 131 ff.` (p. 309) it is the ordinary
  bibliographical "and following".
- **Sigla are NEVER pages of this book.** 31 one- and two-letter sigla are printed in the key at
  pp. 22–24 (folios xix–XXI) — chunk 001 enumerates all 31 and notes that the brief's prose figure
  of 34 disagrees with the brief's own table. Named traps: **`[AD 100]` is not the year A.D. 100**,
  it is ʿAṭṭār's *Dīwān*, poem 100 (p. 49, folio 18) — *"the single most dangerous token in the
  chunk"*; two sigla print in **square** brackets, not parentheses (p. 49); `S. 57` prints a stray
  full stop that makes it look like a German `Seite` (p. 50); `AM no. N` is a hadith number and the
  book says so on PDF 508; **`CV` (p. 330, folio 299) and `DS` (p. 342, folio 311) are not in the
  key and were not expanded** — `DS` looks like a transposed `SD`, *"but it is a guess and it is not
  made here."* `AH Jawahiri` (p. 165, footnote 23) is a capitalised two-letter token that is a
  personal name, not a siglum.
- **The book's own internal cross-references use PRINTED FOLIOS and must never become `(p. N)`.**
  `(see pp. 167-78)` on p. 166 points at PDF 198–209 — chunk 009 calls it *"the single most
  confusable item in the chunk"* because its numbers collide with that chunk's own PDF range.
  Likewise `(p. 221)` on p. 388 (folio 357) and `(N 424; see p. 101)` on p. 461.
- **Footnote reference numbers are glued to the preceding word throughout and several disagree with
  their own notes.** Chunk 022 found three: the marker `sings.83` (p. 416, folio 385) belongs to note
  **63**; `very common.68` (p. 417, folio 386) belongs to note **66**; the note printed `e,i.`
  (p. 411, folio 380) is note **51**. Chunk 017 found a marker printed `3` whose footnote is
  numbered `2.` (p. 320). Chunk 006 found `^S.Mukh., no. 22/5.` where the sequence requires 48 and
  `ffO.Mukh., no. 42/10.` where it requires 50 (p. 112, folio 81). **No footnote number in this book
  should be cited as read**; every footnote is cited by the PDF page it is printed on.
- **Letter-spaced capitals in headings and running headers**, normalised wherever a heading is
  quoted and never quoted as content. The spacing is **inconsistent between facing pages**, so it
  cannot be used as a page signature.
- **Line-break hyphenation**, rejoined silently in every quotation. **Twice the hyphen is LOST**, so
  a naive read yields two false words: `His ene` / `mies` and `excessive asceti` / `cism` (p. 98) —
  and `come into ex istence` (p. 337), `every ani mal` (pp. 338–339).
- **European diacritics are lost totally** in the French, German and Turkish footnotes and are
  quoted as printed throughout. One case matters for content: **`on whose mausoleum in Kenya the
  famous lines are written`** (p. 141, folio 110) — *"the only toponym in the chunk that the text
  layer has corrupted into a different real place."* The same error recurs in an imprint,
  `(Kenya, Turkey, 1956, 1957)` (p. 356, footnote 38), which footnote 39 on p. 357 prints correctly
  as `(Konya, Turkey, 1971)`.
- **Stutter and doubling at the line wrap** (chunk 029, a class the brief does not list):
  `See alsoalao La` (p. 535), `subtle subtlee centers` (p. 535), `See also Nafss` (p. 536),
  `See also Heartrtt` (p. 539), `brought by thethe prescribed religious duties` (p. 539).
- **Two folio facts a human should carry.** **PDF 427 prints `306` where the rule gives 396** — the
  book's one measured offset outlier, recorded on every bullet that cites it as
  `(p. 427, folio 306 as printed — rule gives 396)`. Chunk 023 adds the reason it matters:
  *"the parity self-check does not catch it, because 306 is even and the folio is in leading
  position — so parity passing is not evidence that a folio is right, only that it is not
  transposed."* And **the brief's claim that PDF 440 prints its folio as `44°` is wrong**: p. 440
  prints no folio at all and the string `44°` does not occur in that chunk; the real `44°` is the
  header of **p. 471** (chunk 025).

---

### 4. Thin mappings a human should confirm or cut

One line each. These are the attachments whose whole evidence is named, so a reviewer can weigh the
evidence without re-reading the chunk.

- **shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar** — from a book *title* in a footnote: `The Life and Times of Fartd Ganj-i Shakar` (p. 273, folio 242, chunk 014); Farīd is the *subject* of a cited book, not its author, which the 18 September ruling arguably does not name. *"A human should rule on this case; it is one line to remove."*
- **shrine-of-makhdoom-abdul-rahim-girhori** — from the nisba inside a title, `Umar Muhammad Daudpota, Kaldm-i Girhorl (Hyderabad, Sind, 1956), p. 23.` (p. 147, folio 116, chunk 008; repeated at p. 465 and in the bibliography, chunk 025). **The book never says that `Girhorl` is a person**, never says he is a saint, and never prints `Abdul Rahim`.
- **darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz** — from one Rūmī allegory: `Ayaz, Mahmud of Ghazna's favorite slave` (p. 221, folio 190, chunk 012), plus `his slave Ayaz` / `the Turkish officer Ayaz` (p. 323, folio 292, chunk 017). The book **never** prints `Malik Ahmad Ayaz`, `Malik Ayaz`, Lahore, a governorship, a tomb or a date for him.
- **shrine-of-pir-mangho** — from a parenthesis of ten words with no footnote, no siglum and no saint's name: `(as happened in Mango Pir near Karachi)` (p. 238, folio 207, chunk 013). The identification of the book's `Mango Pir` with the row is the worker's, on the name alone.
- **krishna-mandir-kabari-bazar** and **krishna-mandir-ravi-road** — from a single comparative clause in which Schimmel is explaining an Ibn ʿArabī doctrine and *"the book is not talking about Krishna, Hindu practice, or any temple"* (p. 302, folio 271, chunk 016); reached again from one clause in chunk 025 and from four words inside another entry's gloss, `Gopis (Krishna's cowgirls), 271, 434` (p. 533, chunk 029).
- **tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak** — bare name, and the wrong geography: the whole evidence is `the beautiful mosque Qutbuddln Aybek of Delhi had erected in Ajmer` (p. 377, folio 346, chunk 020). The archive row is in Lahore. **Nine lines later on the same page stands `Qutbuddln Bakhtiyar Kaki from Farghana` (d. 1235), a different man who must not be merged with him.**
- **All ten Peshawar rows** — from the single adjective `Peshawari` in `Arab, Hindu, Turk, or Peshawari` (p. 417, folio 386, chunk 022), inside a paraphrase about transcending identities. The worker: *"a consolidator should consider striking all ten."* See §1.1 for the contrary decision on the same word.
- **shrine-of-lakhi-shah-saddar** — receives the entire Sehwān shrine passage only because its row is located `near Sehwan Sharif`; *"the book never names it, never names Laki or Lakhi, and never names Syed Shah Sadaruddin Lakyari"* (chunk 021).
- **sial-sharif** — from `the Sargodha district` named only as the home of a folk-tale character (p. 419, folio 388, chunk 022).
- **guru-gurpat-mandir-db-80-sirey-ghat** — from Hyderabad named only as a landmark for locating Hala and Jhok; *"a consolidator should weigh how much a row gains from 'Jhok is not far from Hyderabad'"* (chunk 023).
- **gurdwara-pehli-patshahi-jind-pir-sukkur** and **sadh-belo-sadh-belo-island-temple** — from Rohri named only as the home town of two named poets (chunk 023).
- **garh-maharaja-shorkot** — from a bare name, `as Sultan Bahu of the Punjab sang` (p. 200, folio 169, chunk 011): no death date, no tomb, no work named; and from a bare bibliography entry, `Sultan Bahoo. Abyat. Translated by Maqbool Elahi. Lahore, 1967.` (p. 496, folio 465, chunk 026).
- **shrine-of-shah-abdul-karim-bulri** — rests entirely on `Jotwani, Motilal. Shdh cAbdul Karlm. New Delhi, 1970.` (p. 482, folio 451, chunk 026). **The chunk never prints `Bulri`**, gives no date and no place.
- **shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya** and **shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar** — both also rest, in chunk 016, on a bare five-name list with no date, no site and no order, saying only that they `tell of the spiritual glory of the thirteenth century` (pp. 310–311, folios 279–280).
- **shri-panchmukhi-hanuman-mandir-karachi** — from the two-word index entry `Hanuman, 394` (chunk 027).
- **shrine-of-akhund-darweza-baba** — from the index entry `Akhund Darwaza (d. 1638), 398` (chunk 027).
- **tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak** and **shrine-of-sachal-sarmast**, in chunk 027, rest on **cross-references with no locator at all** — the thinnest evidence in the book.
- **Five of chunk 028's thirty-two ids rest on a bare cross-reference and nothing else**, on one line: **shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya**, **rahman-baba-mausoleum-rehman-baba-shrine**, **mazar-of-bulleh-shah**, **shrine-of-shah-abdul-karim-bulri**, **dargah-roza-sufi-shah-inayat-shaheed**.
- **jhollay-lal-mandir** and **darya-lal-mandir-darya-lal-sankat-mochan-mandir** — reached only through the **archive's own** parenthetical `(Jhulelal)`; *"the book never prints Jhulelal in any spelling in this chunk"* (chunk 028). Drop them if that chain is judged too long.
- **shaktipeeth-shri-hinglaj-mata-mandir** — mapped on the archive row's **name**, not on the toponym join: the book's `Hinglaj` is not a substring match against `toponym_map.txt`'s `hingol national park` / `hingol balochistan national park` keys (chunk 023). Flagged so the join is not assumed to have produced it.
- **All eight Multan rows**, from chunk 025, rest on Schimmel's visit to **one unnamed small shrine** — and *"the shrine she describes is a women's shrine that men are not admitted to, and no archive row in Multan is a women's shrine"* (p. 464, folio 433). Weigh that mismatch before the sentence enters an entry for, say, **shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam**.
- **All eight Multan rows**, from chunk 005, rest on a single mention of Multan as Carmathian-held territory that Ḥallāj had visited (p. 98, folio 67) — tenth-century political geography, no shrine, no saint, no building.
- **All five Uch Sharif rows** rest on an orthographic identification the worker made from content, not from the map: the book prints `Ucch` four times (p. 385, folio 354) and the key is `uch sharif`. *"If a consolidator judges the orthographic gap too wide, the five ids fall away but the two person-level rows stand on their own"* (chunk 020).
- **data-darbar** — in chunk 003 it is mapped on *siglum-only* evidence: the name `Hujwiri` is never printed in pp. 59–74; what is printed is the bare `H` three times (`H 103` p. 68; `H 100` twice p. 73), and neither quotation is about Hujwīrī. *"A consolidator may reasonably drop this row from this chunk without losing any content."*
- **mazar-of-bulleh-shah**, **mazar-e-iqbal** and **shrine-of-makhdoom-abdul-rahim-girhori**, in chunk 025, rest on **bare bibliography entries** — thinner than the chat examples the 18 September ruling was made on. *"If the consolidator decides apparatus author entries do not fire the person rule, these three come out and nothing else in the notes changes."*
- **bhit-bhit-shah** — in chunk 011 it rests on a two-step identification the human should ratify: the text names `Shah cAbduDl-Latif in Sind (d. 1752)` (p. 200, folio 169) and a footnote sixteen pages later cites `H. T. Sorley, Shah Abdul Latif of Bhit` (p. 216, folio 185). *"Nothing in the chunk explicitly says that these two are the same man… the agreement is what makes the identification look obvious."*

---

### 5. Placement and attribution doubts

#### 5.1 The `(H …)` Hujwīrī voice test — where the Pass-1 workers split three ways

The rule the chunks applied: a `(H …)` citation is **Hujwīrī's book**, not always **Hujwīrī's
voice**. Three cases arise and the third is the judgement call most worth a human's eye.

- **His own voice → mapped to **data-darbar**.** `H 6` (p. 118, chunk 006), `H 19` / `(Hag)` / `H 180`
  / `H 230` (chunk 008), `H 267` (p. 161), `H 308` (p. 163), `H 278` (p. 177), `H 91, 330` (p. 178)
  (chunk 009), `H 301` (p. 183, folio 152, chunk 010), `H 210` (p. 230, chunk 012), `H 549` —
  Hujwīrī's own paragraph in the first person (p. 321, folio 290, chunk 017).
- **Words he reports from someone else → refused, and parked under "Other".** `H 67` — Ḥamdūn
  al-Qaṣṣār (p. 118, chunk 006); `H 359` — Shiblī's, expressly (p. 132, chunk 007); `H 186` — `one
  of his mystical masters` (p. 160, chunk 008); `H 111` Sarī as-Saqaṭī (p. 162), `H 40` Junayd
  (p. 165), `H 173` Sumnūn (p. 171), `H 158` an unnamed `earlier Sufi` (p. 177), `H 282-83` Junayd
  (p. 177) (chunk 009); `H 293` second occurrence — Shiblī — and `H 458` — a divine address to an
  unnamed mystic (pp. 179, 186, chunk 010); `H 195` (p. 200) and `H 124` (p. 211) (chunk 011);
  `H 235` (p. 237, chunk 012); `H 233-34` (p. 238, folio 207) and `H 302` — Muhammad's experience —
  (p. 249, folio 218), on which chunk 013 **declined to map **data-darbar** at all**; `H 120` —
  Fāṭima of Nishapur (p. 458, chunk 025).
- **NO SPEAKER NAMED AT ALL → handled two contrary ways, and this is the item for a human.**
  **Thirteen such citations.** Chunks 007, 008 and 012 **carried** them as material from his book:
  `H 383`, `H 181` (p. 130), `H 45`, `H 48` (p. 133) — chunk 007 adds that ***"`H 48` is the least
  secure of the four"*** because its framing (`warnings … were not infrequent`) could as easily mean
  Hujwīrī is reporting a current saying; `H 206`, `H 202-204`, `H 138`, `H 321`, `H 303` (chunk 008,
  recorded with speaker `(not stated)`, and ***"a consolidator may wish to demote those five"***);
  `H 228` (p. 234, chunk 012, ***"I carried it, flagged, because refusing it would require assuming
  a speaker the book does not supply"***). Chunk 009 **refused** its three — `H 148` (p. 164),
  `H 404` (p. 166), `H 398` (p. 170) — *"left unattached to any figure rather than assigned to
  Hujwīrī; a human should confirm that refusal."*
  **Same evidence, opposite decisions, thirteen citations, one archive row.**
- **A fourth, quieter case.** Chunk 010 carried `(H 293)` on p. 179 and then said it is **Schimmel's
  own summary** of a position she sources to the *Kashf*, not a quotation of Hujwīrī: *"a
  consolidator may want to downgrade it."*
- **And the live question chunk 002 left open.** Schimmel names Hujwīrī beside only **three** of that
  chunk's `H` citations; **sixteen further `H` sigla** on pp. 45–52 are attached to sayings ascribed
  to Junayd, Shiblī, Yaḥyā ibn Muʿādh and others. Only the three named were mapped. *"A consolidator
  with the key in hand may want to revisit that."*

#### 5.2 A page in a book *about* a saint is not a saying *by* him

**Chunk 013's warning, and it is the most quotable false attribution in the book.** On p. 244
(folio 213) the saying `"It is better to restore one dead heart to eternal life than to restore to
life a thousand dead bodies."` is attributed by Schimmel **only** to `the saint`, with footnote 20
pointing at **Sorley's *Shah Abdul Latif of Bhit*, p. 248**. The worker: *"A page in a book about
Shāh ʿAbdu'l-Laṭīf is not the same as a saying by him; the consolidator must not promote it to his
words."* It is a genuinely attractive sentence and it will be promoted unless someone stops it.

#### 5.3 The `ʿAlī` collision with **data-darbar**'s `principal_figure` string

**data-darbar**'s principal figure is `Hazrat Ali ibn Usman al-Hujwiri (Data Ganj Bakhsh)`, and this
book prints the Prophet's son-in-law as `CA1I`, `CAH`, `cAli`, `CA15` and `AH`. **The collision fires
in at least twelve chunks and was refused every time**: p. 113, folio 82 (chunk 006); p. 138,
folio 107 (chunk 007); pp. 180, 186 (chunk 010); p. 200, folio 169 and footnote 77, p. 206
(chunk 011); p. 230, folio 199 (chunk 012); p. 277, folio 246 and footnote 46, p. 288 and p. 282,
folio 251 (chunk 015); p. 348, folio 317 (chunk 018); pp. 369–371 (chunk 020); five names on one
page in chunk 025 (pp. 458, 469, 475, 480–481); **six different people in chunk 026**; and repeatedly
in chunks 028 and 029. Chunk 026 states the safe rule: of its six, *"only the first —
`Hujwiri, CAH ibn c Uthman al-` (p. 480) — is data-darbar's figure."*

#### 5.4 Other attribution and identity doubts, each left for a human

- **`Shah c lnat` and `Shah clnayat Shahid of Jhok` are kept as two different men, and might be one.**
  Chunk 023: the printed forms differ (`lnat` vs `lnayat`), the centuries differ, and each has its own
  reference (p. 421 and p. 428, folio 397). ***"Given this book's `1`→`l` damage the two names could
  in principle be one mangled name; I do not think so, but a human should confirm, because
  conflating them would move seventeenth-century poetic material onto the Jhok row."*** Chunk 026
  declined to map `Miyen Shah ^Indt jo kaldm` (p. 487, folio 456) for the same reason; chunk 027
  found both entries four lines apart on p. 517 (folio 486) and mapped only
  `lnayat Shahid, Shah (d. 1718)`.
- **`Bilawal` — the book itself declines the identification.** Sachal's litany has `In misfortune
  hast Thou pressed Bilawal` (p. 426, folio 395), and footnote 79 on that very page says
  ***"Karmal and Bilawal have not yet been identified."*** So **shah-noorani-shrine-syed-bilawal-shah-noorani**
  was not mapped (chunk 023), and the bare index entry `Bilawal, 395` (p. 513, folio 482) was not
  mapped either (chunk 027). If a human rules them one man, the material is one clause of a poem
  plus a scholarly non-identification.
- **`Ayaz, 190, 292` in the index was not mapped**, although **darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz** is mapped
  elsewhere: the index prints `Ayaz` with **no gloss whatever** — no Maḥmūd, no Ghazna, no Lahore —
  and prints a **second, clearly different `Ayaz, Sheikh, 390 n` four lines away** (p. 512,
  folio 481, chunk 027).
- **`Omarkot` was not mapped and is the closest near-miss in chunk 023.** The book prints `the mighty
  ruler c Umar of Omarkot` (p. 423, folio 392); the keys are `umarkot` / `umerkot`, with a `u`, and
  the row's own name carries a third variant, `Amarkot`. *"Deciding that the book's `Omarkot` is the
  archive's `Umarkot` would be my geography, not the book's."* Note also that the reference is to a
  ruler's castle **inside a folk tale**, not to the modern town.
- **`Bulrri` was not mapped, and the stakes are high if it is confirmed.** `an unjustified attack on
  Jhok, led by the Sayyids of Bulrri` (p. 428, folio 397): the key is `bulri shah karim` and the
  printed form has a doubled `r`, so the join fails. Chunk 023: *"if a human confirms `Bulrri` =
  Bulri … it would put the two rows **shrine-of-shah-abdul-karim-bulri** and
  **dargah-roza-sufi-shah-inayat-shaheed** on opposite sides of a violent episode."*
- **`Sehwan` was mapped on `shrine_index.tsv`, not on a literal key.** The book prints `Sehwan`,
  never `Sehwan Sharif`; `toponym_map.txt` has no `sehwan` key. Both rows' `location_short` contains
  `Sehwan`, so ruling (a)'s own instruction gives the same two rows (chunk 021). Recorded, not
  smoothed.
- **A silsila conflict on Girhōṛī.** Schimmel calls ʿAbduʾr-Raḥīm Girhōṛī `the eighteenth-century
  Suhrawardi mystic` (p. 418, folio 387); the archive row records a different order (chunk 022).
- **Three quotations in the book have no attributable source and must not be given one.**
  `we may well believe that "no saint excelled him in his devotions and penances."` — of Farīduddīn
  (p. 378, folio 347): *"The book quotes the phrase but names no source for it in the text or in
  footnote 7."* The Chishti couplet `How long will you go to the doors of Amirs and sultans?`
  is attributed only to `One of their poets` (p. 378, folio 347) — **(not stated)**. And the second
  Birge quotation on p. 269 has **no citation**: `(BO 202)` attaches to the first quotation only —
  *"do not silently extend `BO 202` to cover it"* (chunk 014).
- **One inference a worker made from a locator and flagged as theirs.**
  `lmaduddin (disciple of Multani), 353` (p. 517, folio 486): the locator falls inside
  `Baha^uddln Zakariya Multani`'s `352-54`, which is why it was recorded under that row — *"but the
  index does not say which Multānī, and the inference is mine from the locator, not the book's
  statement"* (chunk 027).
- **A conjunction that asserts nothing.** Chunk 023 refuses to say whether Akhund Darwaza had any
  part in the drowning of Pīr-i Roshan's sons (p. 429, folio 398): *"the book joins those clauses
  with `and`, asserts no connection, and neither do I."*
- **A severed sentence that costs the archive a practice name.** Chunk 013 opens mid-sentence at
  `Sati, because they were granted by a living or dead saint to whose threshold they were "sold" by
  the parents).` (p. 238, folio 207). `Sati` is the tail of a list begun on p. 237, in the previous
  chunk. **Child-vowing to a shrine threshold is directly archive-relevant and the practice's name
  is the part that is missing.**
- **A shrine photographed and unnamed.** The plate caption `Woman in trance at a saint's tomb in
  Pakistan.` (p. 182, folio not stated) *"names neither the saint, the tomb nor the town"*
  (chunk 010), and the plate on p. 264 (folio not stated) shows `the founders of the four great
  mystical orders` while naming **none** of the four (chunk 014). Neither can be mapped and neither
  should be guessed at.

---

### 6. What this book does NOT give — stated as a finding

**`Nanak` is absent from the whole `N` run of the `INDEX OF NAMES AND PLACES`, and `Ranjit Singh`
from the `R` run.** Chunk 028 checked both runs directly, because both fall inside its pages: the
`N` run occupies p. 522 (folio 491) and goes `Nadir Shah → Nafahat → Nafisa → Nagorl → Nahifi →
Najmuddm → … → Nwyia` **with no Nanak and no Nankana**; the `R` run occupies pp. 523–524
(folios `4Q2` [text-layer?: 492] and 493) and has no `Ranjit Singh`.

**The consequence for the archive: all 18 gurdwara rows and the samadhi row are unreachable from
this book's own index of names.** That is an absence in the source, not a gap in the notes.

Two corroborating negatives from elsewhere in the back matter:

- Chunk 026 (the bibliography) records that `Mohan Singh (Diwana)`'s two entries (p. 487, folio 456)
  **are Sikh but do not name Guru Nanak**, so the 22 September many-row person rule does not fire and
  *"the 18 gurdwara rows are untouched."*
- Chunk 023 (chapter 8's close) refused to map `the Sikhs` to any gurdwara row: `The language was
  used almost exclusively by the Sikhs as their literary medium` (p. 427), `Ahmad Brelwi's struggle
  against the Sikh (1829-31)` (p. 427), `called in to help against the Sikhs and Mahrattas`
  (p. 431). *"No Guru is named anywhere in this chunk, and `the Sikhs` as a literary community is
  not the **gurdwara-singh-sabha** row's `Sikh community (Singh Sabha)`, which is a specific
  congregation in Quetta."*

**The same check found nine further archive principal figures absent from the index runs it
covers**, recorded by chunk 028 as findings rather than oversights: no `Rukn`/`Rukn-e-Alam` in the
`R` run; no `Musa Pak` and no `Mauj Darya` in the `M` run; no `Sakhi Sarwar`, no `Shah Daula` and no
`Sher Muhammad` in the `S` run; no `Taunsa` or `Sulaiman Taunsvi` in the `T` run; no `Imam Ali-ul-Haq`
in the `I` run.

**And on the Hindu and Sikh side, the index names only two deities in its `I`–`Z` runs — `Krishna`
and `Shiva`.** There is **no `Kali`, no `Durga`, no `Rama`, no `Valmiki`, no `Hanuman`, no `Varuna`,
no `Lakshmi`, no `Gorakhnath` and no `Ratan Nath`** in that range. (`Hanuman, 394` does appear, but
in the earlier run covered by chunk 027 — the two findings are consistent.) So the ten many-row
attachments described in §1.3 are, between them, the whole of what this book's index of names offers
the archive's Hindu, Sikh, Jain and Nanakpanthi rows.

### Consolidation review — worker A (archive sections)

**Counts.** Items promoted out of `## Figures and sites not in the archive` into the archive section:
**0**. Items promoted out of `hedges.md`: **2** of its 206 deferred mappings and declines; **204 were
deliberately left** and are worker C's material. The zero in the first count is a finding, not an
omission: this book's Pass-1 notes were written on 24 September, after all three mapping rules were
ruled, so nothing in `s_other_saints_sites_and_events_not_in_the.md` was parked there under the
pre-21-September convention. Every archive principal figure the book names was already mapped by
Pass 1, and every toponym in that file that has rows in `toponym_map.txt` is either an imprint, a bare
index entry, a demonym or a spelling variant — all of which the brief tells me not to promote.

#### The two promotions, and why each is covered by a ruled rule

1. **`data darbar` → 2 rows (**darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz**, **peer-makki**), ruling (a).** Chunk 006 declined
   the key because `The book prints Data Ganj Bakhsh, not Data Darbar.` That is an **orthographic**
   objection, not one of the three unruled questions, and it is exactly the class of gap that Pass-1
   workers on this same book fired through twice elsewhere: chunk 020 joined the book's `Ucch` to the
   key `uch sharif`, and chunk 021 joined the book's bare `Sehwan` to the key `sehwan sharif` by going
   to `location_short` as ruling (a) directs. Both declined rows name this shrine in their own
   `location_short` (`Darbar Ali Hajveri Ganj Bakhsh (Data Darbar)`; `Bhati Gate area (near Data
   Darbar)`), and the book makes two substantive statements about the shrine as a place of pilgrimage
   (p. 119, folio 88; p. 376, folio 345). **Both rows are already inside the Lahore 35, so reversing
   this costs nothing.**
2. **The index entry `Ayaz, 190, 292` → **darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz**, ordinary person rule.** Chunk 027
   declined it because `Identity cannot be established from this chunk` — an identity objection, not
   the bare-index-entry objection, and chunk 027 mapped thirteen other bare index person entries
   without hesitation. A consolidator can see what a chunk worker could not: the entry's two locators
   are precisely the two printed folios at which this book discusses Maḥmūd's Ayāz (p. 221 = folio 190,
   p. 323 = folio 292), both already mapped to this row by chunks 012 and 017. **The second, clearly
   different entry `Ayaz, Sheikh, 390 n` four lines away is NOT mapped.**

#### Where a ruled rule looks wrong on this book's evidence — Rauf's to decide, not mine

- **REVIEW: the `peshawar` firing is ten rows resting on one adjective, and a demonym is one of the
  three unruled questions.** The whole evidence is `the Sufi is no longer Arab, Hindu, Turk, or
  Peshawari` (p. 417, folio 386). The book says nothing else about Peshawar or about any Peshawar site
  anywhere in 543 pages; the only other occurrences are one imprint and the index's own `Peshawari, 386`.
  Chunk 022 fired it under ruling (a) and said in the same breath that **a consolidator should consider
  striking all ten**; chunk 028 refused the identical token as a nisba. I have carried the firing rather
  than reverse a Pass-1 mapping, but **this is the single attachment in the document I would strike if
  Rauf rules that a demonym does not fire ruling (a).**
- **REVIEW: `"Les entretiens de Lahore."` is a toponym inside an article title, and the Pass-1 worker
  who fired it said they were not comfortable doing so.** §6(ii) exempts imprints and index locators;
  an article title is neither, so ruling (a) as written fires and takes all 35 Lahore rows on the fact
  that Massignon and Huart published an article with that title in 1926 (p. 486, folio 455). **Lahore
  is carried on four genuinely substantive statements regardless**, so nothing turns on it for this
  book — but the sub-case wants ruling for the corpus.
- **REVIEW: the bare-index-entry rule and the many-row person rule pull against each other on identical
  evidence, and only in the index.** Chunk 028 declined every toponym headword in the
  `INDEX OF NAMES AND PLACES` — `Lahore, 88, 225, 239, 345, 361, 384, 388`; `Multan, 67, 82, 344, 352,
  353, 354, 433`; `Karachi, 207`; `Sialkot, 367 .`; `Rohri, 396, 397`; `Sargodha, 388`;
  `Pakpattan, 346, 348, 384`; `Jhok, 395, 397` — and in the same chunk **mapped `Krishna, 271, 434` and
  `Shiva, 355`, which are equally bare headwords, because the many-row person rule takes 2 and 8 rows.**
  Ten of this document's ninety-nine ids depend on that asymmetry. I have carried it exactly as Pass 1
  left it; **it is the first thing to ask Rauf.**
- **REVIEW: two index entries are NOT bare pointers, and the decline may be wrong on them
  specifically.** `Mela chiraghdn (festival in Lahore), 384` (p. 536, folio 505) and
  `Mdlang (dervish at the sanctuary in Sehwan), 355` (p. 536, folio 505) are declined as index entries —
  but **the book's own gloss, not the locator, is what states that the festival is in Lahore and that
  there are mālang at the sanctuary in Sehwan.** That is place-tradition material of exactly the kind
  ruling (a) exists to catch. Both places are carried on other evidence in this document, so nothing is
  lost either way, but the reasoning is worth ruling on.
- **REVIEW: `Hinglaj, 390` (p. 516, folio 485) is the likeliest of the five declined bare index place
  entries to be reversed**, because it matches an archive row's own **name** rather than a
  `toponym_map.txt` key, and because its locator `390` sits in the same folio run as `Bhit Shah`, `Hala`
  and `Abdu :l l-Karim Bulrrrwaro`. The row is reached from the body text anyway (p. 421, folio 390).
  The other four declined index place entries — `Bhit Shah, 383, 390`, `Hala, 390`,
  `Hyderabad Sind, 372 n, 390, 397`, `Bulrri, 397` — each resolve to a row already reached by the person
  rule, so **declining them costs the archive nothing in this book.**
- **REVIEW: what the missing province key costs, stated plainly.** `toponym_map.txt` has no `sind`,
  `sindh`, `punjab`, `balochistan` or `kashmir` key while roughly thirty archive rows carry Sindh in
  `location_short`. The material this loses is not marginal: Ḥallāj's journey through Sind and the claim
  that `The seeds he sowed there grew in later centuries in the mystical poetry of this province.`
  (p. 98, folio 67; p. 106, folio 75; p. 107, folio 76); the hundred flag-hung trees of Sind called after
  Gīlānī, with their vows and sweetmeats (p. 279, folio 248); `Proper names like Ghauth Bakhsh … in Sind
  and Balochistan` (p. 279, folio 248); `Old Sindhi songs describe how his spiritual realm extends from
  Istanbul to Delhi` (p. 279, folio 248); the women saints of Sind, the `haft cafifa` and the
  `mukhaddardt-i abddliyya` (p. 465, folio 434); the millstone saint and the Hindu physician (pp. 238,
  240); the conquest of 711 (p. 61, folio 30); and the whole bridal-soul argument `from Sind to Kashmir`
  (p. 465, folio 434). **Chunks 015 and 025 reach zero archive ids because of this.** No join was
  invented. If Rauf wants province-level firing, this book is where it would pay most.
- **REVIEW: what the imprint-city decline costs, stated plainly.** Twenty-three independent Pass-1
  readings declined it. Cumulatively that is roughly **fifty `Lahore` imprints (35 rows each), forty
  `Karachi` (11 rows), sixteen `Hyderabad, Sind` (1 row), two `Peshawar` (10 rows) and one `Multan`
  (8 rows)** across chunks 001, 004–019, 021–026. Chunk 026 alone is twenty-three pages of bibliography
  firing `lahore` 20 times, `karachi` 13 and `hyderabad` 12. **Keeping the decline is right on the
  evidence** — an imprint carries no material about the place — and reversing it would attach "a 1966
  book was printed here" to dozens of rows dozens of times. Recorded so the size of the decision is
  visible.

#### Judgement calls a human must make before any of this enters an entry

- **`Omarkot` vs `umarkot`/`umerkot`.** The book prints `the mighty ruler c Umar of Omarkot` (p. 423,
  folio 392) and indexes `Omarkot, 392`. Two archive rows are keyed `umarkot`
  (**shrine-of-makhdoom-abdul-rahim-girhori**) and `umerkot` (**umarkot-amarkot-shiv-mandir**, whose own
  name carries a third variant, `Amarkot`). The substring join fails on the `O`. **Not promoted** — it
  is a spelling call, and the reference is to a ruler's castle inside a folk tale, not to the modern
  town. A human should rule.
- **`Bulrri` vs `bulri shah karim`.** `an unjustified attack on Jhok, led by the Sayyids of Bulrri`
  (p. 428, folio 397), and the index's `Bulrri, 397`. **Not promoted** — the printed form has a doubled
  `r` and the join fails. If a human confirms `Bulrri` = Bulri, the material becomes notable: **it would
  put **shrine-of-shah-abdul-karim-bulri** and **dargah-roza-sufi-shah-inayat-shaheed** on opposite sides of
  a violent episode.** The sentence is already carried under the Jhok row, so nothing is lost meanwhile.
- **`Bilawal, 395`.** The archive has `shah-noorani-shrine-syed-bilawal-shah-noorani`. **Not promoted,
  on the book's own authority**: footnote 79 on the very page says `Karmal and Bilawal have not yet been
  identified.` (p. 426, folio 395). Making the identification would be an addition the book refuses to
  make.
- **The three-id `Lai Udero` chain.** **shrine-at-odero-lal-udero-lal-teerath-asthan** is direct — the
  book's own `Tahir, Sheikh. See Lai Udero` reproduces that row's `Sheikh Tahir / Udero Lal` pairing.
  ****jhollay-lal-mandir** and **darya-lal-mandir-darya-lal-sankat-mochan-mandir** are reached only through
  the archive's own parenthetical `(Jhulelal)`; the book never prints `Jhulelal` in any spelling.** Drop
  those two if that chain is judged too long.
- **The Multan women's shrine does not fit any Multan row.** Schimmel's `one such small shrine in
  Multan, decorated with lovely blue and white tiles` is explicitly **a women's shrine that men are not
  admitted to** (p. 464, folio 433). **No archive row in Multan is a women's shrine.** Ruling (a)
  attaches it to all eight; weigh that before letting the sentence into an entry for, say,
  **shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam**.
- **Two attachments rest on a single bare token each and should be read as navigational only**:
  **shri-panchmukhi-hanuman-mandir-karachi**, whose entire evidence is the index headword `Hanuman, 394`
  (p. 516, folio 485); and **sial-sharif**, whose entire evidence is `the son of a landlord of the
  Sargodha district` inside the plot of a folk tale (p. 419, folio 388).
- **A silsila conflict with the archive.** The book calls ʿAbduʾr-Raḥīm Girhōrī `the eighteenth-century
  Suhrawardi mystic` (p. 418, folio 387); **the archive row records Naqshbandi.** Both are kept. Related:
  **shrine-of-makhdoom-nooh-hala** is placed by the book among `mystical leaders of the Suhrawardiyya` at
  Hala (p. 421, folio 390), which **agrees** with its archive row.
- **A silsila join was declined and is not a ruled rule.** Chunk 015 records that the archive has
  thirteen rows whose `silsila` is Suhrawardi and that pp. 275–276 and 278–279 are the book's account of
  how the Suhrawardiyya and Qadiriyya began. **No settled ruling licenses attaching an order's founding
  narrative to every row of that silsila**, so nothing was attached. If Rauf rules otherwise, that
  material is already assembled and page-referenced in section C above.
- **`Shah ʿInāt` and `Shāh ʿInāyat Shahīd` were deliberately kept apart.** The book's index headword for
  the Jhok martyr carries his own epithet `Shahid`, while `lnat, Mian Shah (d. early i8th century), 39°`
  (p. 517, folio 486) and `Miyen Shah ^Indt jo kaldm` (p. 487, folio 456) are given no such epithet and
  are dated to the early eighteenth century as a Sindhi poet closely related to Laṭīf. **A human should
  decide whether the two entries name one man.**
- **The index columns collide on PDF 536, and that page is flagged for re-checking against the image.**
  Chunk 028 also records column bleed elsewhere in the index — `Khaqani … 16,` and `Krishna, 271, 434`
  share line space on p. 519. **A human re-checking `Krishna`, `Mango Pir` and `Shiva` should confirm
  the column assignment**, since all three are short entries in a crowded right column and all three
  carry archive ids.
- **Every death year in this document that comes from the index sits inside a parenthesis in a
  digit-damaged apparatus**, so the book's side of each contradiction is itself provisional. The
  contradictions with the archive, collected:

  | book | the book's year | archive row | archive |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `Hujwiri … (d. ca. 1071)` / `he died there in 1071` | ca. 1071 | **data-darbar** | 1072 |
  | `Fariduddin Ganj-i Shakar (d. 1265)` | 1265 | **shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar** | 1266 |
  | `Baha^uddln Zakariya Multani (d. ca. 1267)` / `(d. ca. 1262)` | ca. 1262 and ca. 1267 | **shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya** | 1267 |
  | `Jalaluddln Surkhpush (d. 1292)` | 1292 | **shrine-of-jalaluddin-surkh-posh-bukhari-jalaluddin-bukhari** | 1291 |
  | `Jalaluddln Makhdum-i Jahaniyan (d. 1382-83)` / `(d. 1383)` | 1382-83 and 1383 | **shrine-of-makhdoom-jahaniyan-jahangasht** | 1384 |
  | `Lai Shahbaz Qalandar (d. 1262)` | 1262 | **lal-shahbaz-qalandar** | 1274 |
  | `Madho Lai Husayn (d. 1593)` / `(1539-93)` | 1593 | **madho-lal-hussain** | 1599 |
  | `Qutbuddln Aybek (d. 1206)` | 1206 | **tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak** | 1210 |
  | `Sachal Sarmast (d. 1826)` / `(i 739-1826)` | 1826 | **shrine-of-sachal-sarmast** | 1827 |
  | `^bdu^r-Rahman (Rahman Baba) (d. 1709)` | 1709 | **rahman-baba-mausoleum-rehman-baba-shrine** | 1711 |
  | `Makhdum Nuh (i7th century)` | 17th century | **shrine-of-makhdoom-nooh-hala** | 1590 |

  The last of these is a century-level disagreement and is larger than any of the others. The book
  **agrees** with the archive on `Akhund Darwaza (d. 1638)`, `Girhori, ^bduVRahlm (d. 1778)`,
  `lnayat Shahid, Shah (d. 1718)`, `Abdu :l l-Karim Bulrrrwaro (d. 1623)`, `Sultan Bahu (d. 1691)`,
  `Mian Mir (d. 1635)` and `Iqbal, Sir Muhammad (d. 1938)`; it gives **no** death year for Warith Shah,
  where the archive records 1798.
- **One internal contradiction inside the book itself, not with the archive**: `Qadi Qadan (d. 1546)` in
  the running text (p. 420, folio 389) against `Qadl Qadan (d. 1551), 389` in the index (p. 523,
  folio `4Q2`). Both are recorded side by side.
- ****data-darbar** rests on the siglum `H` alone in one chunk.** Across pp. 59–74 the book cites `H`
  three times and **never prints the name `Hujwiri`**; the attachment there rests on the abbreviations
  key printed on PDF 22–24, outside that chunk. A Pass-1 worker set this out as a judgement a
  consolidator may reasonably reverse; I have kept it, because the key is the book's own.
- **`Guru Nanak is absent from the book.`** The 22 September many-row rule would have given him
  eighteen gurdwara rows, and he is not in the `N` run of the index; `Ranjit Singh` is not in the `R`
  run. That is a real finding about this book and is recorded as an explicit negative above rather
  than left as an empty result.

### Consolidation review — worker B (cross-cutting sections)

**Count of distinct sub-topics merged: 251**, grouped under **20 sub-headings**. Every sub-topic is a bullet that merges what the 29 Pass-1 chunk notes say about one institution, practice, argument or apparatus item, carrying all of that item's page references; where two chunks gave two readings of the same thing, both are kept rather than reconciled.

**Archive ids carried: 53 distinct rows.** Lahore 35 (twice, at place level, for Melā Chirāghān and for Hujwiri's tomb as a place of pilgrimage) · Multan 8 (twice, for Iraqi's poems sung at the tomb door and for the greedy women guardians) · **lal-shahbaz-qalandar**, **shrine-of-lakhi-shah-saddar** · **bhit-bhit-shah** · **dargah-roza-sufi-shah-inayat-shaheed** · **rahman-baba-mausoleum-rehman-baba-shrine** · **shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar** · **shrine-of-sachal-sarmast** · **mazar-of-bulleh-shah** · **shrine-of-makhdoom-abdul-rahim-girhori** · **guru-gurpat-mandir-db-80-sirey-ghat**. Three of those (**data-darbar**, **madho-lal-hussain**, **mazar-e-iqbal**) are also inside the Lahore 35 and take their material at person level as well as place level.

**Ruled rules that this book's evidence complicates. None was deviated from; each is reported.**

- **REVIEW: the `sehwan sharif` key versus the book's bare `Sehwan`.** The book prints `Sehwan` bare three times — the "fair," the malangs, and the index gloss `Mdlang (dervish at the sanctuary in Sehwan), 355` — while `toponym_map.txt`'s only key is `sehwan sharif` (2 rows), the two rows' `location_short` values being "Sehwan Sharif, Jamshoro District, Sindh" and "Laki (Lakhi), near Sehwan Sharif, Jamshoro, Sindh, Pakistan". I fired ruling (a) on the two rows, reading `Sehwan` and `Sehwan Sharif` as the same place. That is a judgement about a shortened place name, not a province join, so I do not think it touches open question 2 — but it is not the same as a key matching verbatim, and Rauf may want it stated as a rule ("a toponym that is the head of a `location_short` fires, even without the honorific"). If he declines it, the two ids come off one line.
- **REVIEW: the imprint-city decline does not cleanly cover an institution's seat.** I declined every publisher's imprint (open question 1) including `Multan, ca. 1976` on the Shackle entry, `Lahore, 1960` on the Bullhe Shah *Dīwān*, and `Karachi` on `AP` and `QR` in the sigla key. But `The Shah Waliul-lah Academy in Hyderabad, Sind, under the presidency of Maulana Ghulam Mustafa Qasimi, has published…` (p. 403, folio 372, footnote 41) is a toponym in running prose naming where a scholarly body sits, not an imprint line — so I **fired** ruling (a) on it (1 row). It is a publishing body, so it sits adjacent to the declined case, and this is the third sub-case of open question 1 after the article-title case (`"Les entretiens de Lahore."`) and the name-element/demonym case. It wants ruling with them.
- **REVIEW: the bare-index-entry tension (open question 3) is *not* reproducible from this book's Index of Subjects, and that is worth recording.** Unlike the Index of Names and Places, the Index of Subjects' entries carry the book's **own parenthetical glosses**, so they are statements, not pointers: `Mela chiraghdn (festival in Lahore), 384`, `Mdlang (dervish at the sanctuary in Sehwan), 355`, `Jagir (India) (endowment of land), 347`, `Waqf (tax-exempt endowment), 238`, `Dargdh (dervish convent), 232, 238, 360, 397`. The first two name places and I fired (a) on them on the strength of the gloss, not of the headword. If Rauf rules that bare index entries never fire, these two should be re-examined separately from the name-index case, because the evidence is different in kind.
- **REVIEW: open question 2 costs this book heavily and measurably.** Its two densest Pakistani practice passages outside chapter 8 reach **zero archive ids** because `toponym_map.txt` has no `sind`/`sindh`/`punjab` key: the vows, flags and sweetmeats at the Sind trees (p. 279, folio 248), and `The province of Sind, generally famous for its saint worship, is particularly rich in legends connected with women saints` plus women rising to the rank of murshid there (p. 464, folio 433). Add `In all the provinces of Muslim India and Pakistan one may see shrines of women saints to which men are not admitted` (p. 464, folio 433) and the whole Punjab/Sind literary-region argument (pp. 414–418, folios 383–387). None of it is invented a join for.
- **REVIEW: ruling (a) fired at place level is, in this book, frequently *more* informative than a site-level attachment would be, and once it is misleadingly so.** `Iraqi's poems are still being sung by Pakistani musicians at the door of the master's tomb in Multan` (p. 384, folio 353) attaches to all 8 Multan rows including **prahladpuri-temple**, a Hindu temple, and the sentence is manifestly about one Muslim dargah whose master the book does not name. The ruling is correct as ruled and I have not deviated; an entry writer should read the bullet, not the head.
- **REVIEW: one attachment made under the 18 September person rule rests on a bibliography title alone.** **shrine-of-makhdoom-abdul-rahim-girhori** takes `Daudpota, Umar Muhammad. Kalam-i Girhorl. Hyderabad, Sind, 1956. In Sindhi.` (p. 475, folio 444). The rule admits it; the evidence is a title and nothing else, and the bullet says so.

**Things a human must check.**

1. **`p. 427` prints folio `306` where the rule `folio = PDF − 31` gives `396`.** Four bullets in this file depend on that page (Naqshbandīs and Ḥallāj; the 1843/1852 Sindhi language and print note; the Panjabi-to-Sikh transfer; the *hurr* disqualification). Both readings are printed side by side throughout. **This is the only apparent folio-rule violation anywhere in my sources** and is far likelier to be a transcription slip on `396` than a real exception — it needs one look at the image.
2. **Two accounts of the enlarged German edition, and they disagree twice.** Ernst gives `1985` and `Munich: Eugen DiederichsVerlag` (p. 20, folio XVII); Schimmel's own Addendum gives `(Cologne, 1986)` (p. 500, folio 469). Year and city both differ. Neither is resolved.
3. **`CV` is used once as a siglum (`CV 135`, p. 330, folio 299) and is not in the 31-entry key.** It is not expanded here and must not be expanded on guess.
4. **The key holds 31 sigla, not 34**, against the working assumption. Worth reconciling with whatever produced the "34".
5. **Arnold's article page range disagrees with itself** — `11:67-73` in footnote 12 (p. 270, folio 239) against `vol. 11, pp. 68-73` in the bibliography (p. 470, folio 439) — as does its title (`"Saints, Muhammadan, in India"` / `"Saints, Muhammadan, India"`).
6. **`Aczam Tattawi`'s title and editor print two ways** — `Tuhfat at-tahirln` / `ed. Badr-i Alam Durrani` (p. 471, folio `44°`) against `Tuhfat at-tdhirin` / `ed. B. A. Durrani` (p. 465, folio 434) — and **Jamal Abro's German title two ways**, `"Geschwarzten Gesichts"` (p. 468, folio 437) against `"Geschwarzten Gesichtes"` (p. 429, folio 398).
7. **Dermenghem's surname prints three ways** (`Dermenghem` p. 233; `Derraenghem` p. 438; `Dermenghem, fimile` p. 475) and the first name's initial is damaged in all three. Not reconstructed.
8. **Asín Palacios is dated `1933` in the text (p. 284, folio 253) while footnote 39 cites `"Sadilies y alumbrados," Al-Andalus 9 (1944), 16 (1951)`** — a date tension inside one page's apparatus.
9. **`Bertels` in the text against `Y. E. Berthels` in footnote 17 on the same page** (p. 332, folio 301).
10. **The two Suhrawardī handbooks are indexed at the identical opening locator `122, 245`** (pp. 511–512, folios 480–481) — plausible, but worth one check against the image because the columns of the back matter are the damaged region.
11. **`Sanaa's` = Sanāʾī's, `denned` = defined, `Suns`/`Sunsm` = Sufis/Sufism, `Carnal` = ʿamal, `very limb` = every limb.** Every one of these is a real English word and none of them was silently corrected. `Sunsm` appears in this file twice (p. 54, folio 23 and p. 434, folio 403) and `Sanaa's` must never be allowed to become a place.
12. **`AM no. N` is a hadith number in Furūzānfar, `B 37` is a paragraph in Baqlī, `MM 108-10` is a page in Molé, `(see Chapter 4)` and `(see Appendix 2)` are the book's own internal cross-references, and every number inside an index entry is a damaged printed folio.** None of them is a page of this book and none was converted.
